Before links, read [DEFINITION_LINKS.md#links-between-definitions](DEFINITION_LINKS.md#links-between-definitions). Before authoring, read [../ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md#design-simplicity](../ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md#design-simplicity) and the exact type checklist linked in this file.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"fef13ff697eca3188c35b224644f1baf22534d9cb30142f440882d170d28bc6c\"}, {\"heading\": \"draft-any-missing-definitions\", \"title\": \"Draft any missing definitions\", \"anchor\": \"draft-any-missing-definitions\", \"start_line\": 29, \"end_line\": 108, \"content\": \"### Draft any missing definitions\\n\\n**Inputs:** The confirmed outcome, scope limits, and missing-definition list\\nfrom [Identify the existing artifacts that apply to the change](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#identify-the-existing-artifacts-that-apply-to-the-change).\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** New drafts of the missing intents (INT), capabilities\\n  (CAP), requirements (REQ), specifications (SPEC), applicable architectures\\n  (ARCH), and required architecture decisions (ADR). None if no definitions\\n  are missing.\\n- **Domain files and directories:** The structure reported by\\n  `scaffold-domain`, created only if the selected domain is missing.\\n- **Transient working material:** The inventory updated with generated IDs\\n  and file paths. Retain it under the same conditions as [Describe the intended outcome](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#describe-the-intended-outcome).\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Read `Design simplicity` in `docs/engineering/ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md`.\\n2. Select the domain for each missing definition from the inventory. For a\\n   missing domain, preview its creation with the domain commands below.\\n3. Inspect the proposed domain paths. Resolve any reported conflict before\\n   running the domain creation command.\\n4. Select the first missing artifact type from the table below. Skip types\\n   already covered by unchanged existing artifacts.\\n5. Run the artifact preview command. Inspect its type, destination path, and\\n   `allocated_id`.\\n6. Run the artifact creation command with that exact ID. If a conflict occurs,\\n   repeat the preview; do not overwrite an existing record.\\n7. Open the returned file. Complete its title, owners, dates, content, and\\n   declared links. Replace all template placeholders.\\n8. Apply `### Checklist` under the matching `## TYPE` heading in\\n   `docs/engineering/ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md`.\\n9. Read the completed draft. Record its ID and path in the inventory.\\n10. Repeat actions 4\\u20139 for each remaining missing definition.\\n\\n| Order | Artifact | TYPE | Content | Declared links |\\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\\n| 1 | Intent (INT) | `intent` | Problem, intended outcome, affected users, and scope limits. | None required here. |\\n| 2 | Capability (CAP) | `capability` | The ability needed to achieve the outcome. | `derives_from` \\u2192 intent (INT). |\\n| 3 | Requirement (REQ) | `requirement` | Observable behavior, conditions, and acceptance criteria. | `derives_from` \\u2192 capability (CAP). |\\n| 4 | Specification (SPEC) | `specification` | Detailed behavior, constraints, failure behavior, and checkable examples. | `specifies` \\u2192 requirement (REQ). |\\n| 5 | Architecture (ARCH), when applicable | `architecture` | Components, boundaries, and the architecture decision assessment. | `addresses` \\u2192 requirement (REQ); `conforms_to` \\u2192 specification (SPEC). |\\n| 6 | Architecture decision (ADR), when required | `adr` | Context, alternatives, chosen design, and consequences. | `decides` \\u2192 architecture (ARCH). |\\n\\nRecord an unresolved design choice for [Record unresolved decisions (DEC)](RISKS_AND_DECISIONS.md#record-unresolved-decisions-dec). Do not present a recommendation\\nas an agreed architecture decision (ADR). Prepare verification contracts (VER)\\nin [Define how the result will be verified](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#define-how-the-result-will-be-verified).\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\nFor a missing domain, use the first command in action 2 and the second only\\nafter reviewing the preview in action 3:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl scaffold-domain REPO --domain DOMAIN --dry-run --json\\nharnessctl scaffold-domain REPO --domain DOMAIN --json\\n```\\n\\nFor each missing artifact, use this **creation sequence** in actions 5\\u20136.\\nReplace `TYPE` with the table value. Replace `ARTIFACT-ID` in the second\\ncommand with the first command's `allocated_id`:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl create-artifact REPO --domain DOMAIN --type TYPE --dry-run --json\\nharnessctl create-artifact REPO --domain DOMAIN --type TYPE --id ARTIFACT-ID --json\\n```\\n\\nThe preview writes nothing and does not reserve an ID. Creation writes one\\nincomplete template and returns its path. Content completion is a separate\\nediting action. The JSON output does not contain the authoring checklist.\\n\\n**Completion:** Each missing definition has a completed draft with an ID and\\npath. Unresolved choices are identified. If no definitions are missing,\\nrecord `None needed` in the transient inventory.\\n\\n**Later use:** These definitions supply the verification contracts (VER) in\\n[Define how the result will be verified](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#define-how-the-result-will-be-verified) and the governing links in work orders (WO) in [Prepare work orders (WO) linking the definitions, scope, planned work, and verification requirements](DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md#prepare-work-orders-wo-linking-the-definitions-scope-planned-work-and-verification-requirements). Their acceptance\\nis handled in the authorization process.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"c38865671a81429dd291d9f9bb333deae02a4098d4afcf034f293ee919e990e0\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-digest39-20261012\\\\claude-351\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"71de2cc92a470e2da25d525e3ad002b77faedb6ef0e69e2fa476a2fa4cb78ff5\", \"bytes\": 11691, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"design-simplicity\", \"title\": \"Design simplicity\", \"anchor\": \"design-simplicity\", \"start_line\": 21, \"end_line\": 58, \"content\": \"## Design simplicity\\n\\nFor unfinished work, `harnessctl validate-draft REPO --artifact ID --json`\\nreports whether one supported draft has an admissible shape. Only exact values\\nat the same type and field in this evaluator's canonical template, and missing\\nor empty required authoring links, can be reported as incomplete. Invalid\\nidentity, dates, metadata shapes, actual endpoints, lifecycle history and\\ndisposition are errors. A missing non-template ID is an error. Background\\nfindings remain visible; this result makes no whole-repository validity claim.\\nComplete the authoring checklist and pass ordinary approval gates before\\nrequesting approval. Draft admission grants no authority to write or approve.\\n\\nChoose the least complex design that satisfies the agreed requirements and\\nconstraints. Justify material added complexity by the need it serves and why\\na simpler approach is insufficient. Consider the effort to understand, build,\\ntest, operate and change the whole solution. Defer speculative needs. Review\\nboth the necessity of the specified behavior and the simplicity of its\\nimplementation. Preserve correctness and the project's required quality and\\nsafety properties.\\n\\nStart from the smallest complete solution. Each project supplies its agreed\\nusers, scale, operating conditions and quality constraints. Agreed future needs\\ncan matter; imagined flexibility alone is insufficient. Credible risk analysis\\ncan justify prevention before an incident. Fewer lines, shorter specifications\\nor fewer tests do not establish simplicity; moving work to users, operations\\nor dependencies also has a cost.\\n\\nApply the relevant questions below before definition approval and during design\\nand implementation review. Return unjustified obligations or design complexity\\nfor revision through the existing decision process. Explain material choices\\nin the existing specification, design or review: the need, why a simpler option\\nis insufficient and the added cost. Scale the explanation to the decision; a\\nsmall choice may need one sentence, a significant design may use its existing\\narchitecture decision. Trivial changes need no separate comparison document.\\n\\nThese are human review standards. They introduce no separate KISS artifact,\\nmandatory score, receipt, automated simplicity gate or additional approval.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"29e9b6296f39215c4b2ad36659c0674f0b20650682db9aa1187fc97871cfdb06\"}, {\"heading\": \"intent\", \"title\": \"intent\", \"anchor\": \"intent\", \"start_line\": 76, \"end_line\": 84, \"content\": \"## intent\\n\\n### Checklist\\n\\n- Explain the present problem, who it affects, and the outcome wanted.\\n- What is the smallest useful outcome, and which needs and constraints are actually agreed?\\n- Say how the owner will know the change helped; an honest baseline may be `not measured`.\\n- State the important scope limits. Create a new intent when the desired outcome changes.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"291e8274f0c400a3d2d1ecdd56f2c10d68ab6cb40f667076a034e44d87c05e42\"}, {\"heading\": \"requirement\", \"title\": \"requirement\", \"anchor\": \"requirement\", \"start_line\": 59, \"end_line\": 75, \"content\": \"## requirement\\n\\n### Checklist\\n\\n- State the observable behavior and why it is needed. Ordinary language is accepted; `SHALL` is optional.\\n- What agreed outcome does this obligation serve, for whom and under which conditions? Separate requirements from assumptions and speculative needs.\\n- Include a concrete acceptance condition in `Examples`, `Acceptance`, `measure`, `verification_notes`, or a linked verification contract.\\n- Name the source capability and a verification method. Supported method arrays contain `test`, `analysis`, `inspection`, or `demonstration`; retained text methods remain readable.\\n- Replace unfinished placeholders and resolve blocking decisions before approval.\\n\\n### Guidance\\n\\nWrite enough for someone to implement and check the behavior. Use the template's\\nsections when helpful. Word counts, sentence shapes and numbers of code references\\nare not checked. Writing hints never block approval. Optional metadata must still\\nhave a valid value when supplied.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"333872b200842af881c6c22cfcb0631897071421f423885b2702ac6b72c75770\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/AUTHORIZE_WORK.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-digest39-20261012\\\\claude-351\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\AUTHORIZE_WORK.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"991b526d6280554dcca6901c1790e5519b8894e08635a1f10618aa84a29aaf28\", \"bytes\": 15212, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"read-this-when\", \"title\": \"Read this when\", \"anchor\": \"read-this-when\", \"start_line\": 3, \"end_line\": 6, \"entry_condition\": true, \"content\": \"## Read this when\\n\\nThe proposed package needs validation, review or a human authorization decision.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"8b18d925f39f314acc9ea6bcc9820015c4c9430d836144e85bc31b670752371b\"}, {\"heading\": \"before-this-action\", \"title\": \"Before this action\", \"anchor\": \"before-this-action\", \"start_line\": 7, \"end_line\": 10, \"entry_condition\": true, \"content\": \"## Before this action\\n\\nFor reviewing each definition, use its selected [Type checklist](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#type-checklists). Before review, read [AUTHORITY.md#decision-rights](AUTHORITY.md#decision-rights) and [AUTHORITY.md#delegation-and-separation](AUTHORITY.md#delegation-and-separation). Before approving work, read [WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#assurance-classification](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#assurance-classification). For an open or deferred decision, read [RISKS_AND_DECISIONS.md#blocking-decisions](RISKS_AND_DECISIONS.md#blocking-decisions).\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"9aa32693606102a5aa74b2e4a614aed6904471e5f618dea93356baac97c86ca2\"}, {\"heading\": \"assess-the-proposed-artifact-package\", \"title\": \"Assess the proposed artifact package\", \"anchor\": \"assess-the-proposed-artifact-package\", \"start_line\": 32, \"end_line\": 83, \"content\": \"### Assess the proposed artifact package\\n\\n**Inputs:** The selected work order (WO) IDs, proposed definitions, validation\\nresults, and existing decision records (DEC).\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** None created or modified.\\n- **Transient working material:** A review list identifying the selected\\n  artifacts, their states, applicable checks, and reported blockers.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Run validation for the repository.\\n2. Inspect each selected work order (WO) with the command below.\\n3. Read the work order (WO) and its returned reading manifest.\\n4. Review the governing definitions against the relevant checklists in\\n   `docs/engineering/ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md`.\\n5. Check that the proposed work has defined scope, verification coverage,\\n   assurance classification, and accountable humans for the required decisions.\\n6. List the blockers affecting the selected package. Keep unrelated findings\\n   separate.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl validate REPO --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --checkpoint transition --target approved --json\\n```\\n\\nRead the result of each required check. A `pass` satisfies that check only;\\nit does not approve the artifacts or authorize implementation. If a required\\ncheck returns `fail` or `not_assessable`, report its identifier and stated\\nreason, then follow \\u201cIf a procedure is blocked.\\u201d Do not proceed with the\\naffected approval.\\n\\nThe last command evaluates work-order approval gates. A checkpoint-free\\n`check` only reports lifecycle context. Definition artifacts cannot be selected\\nwith `check`; their transition gates are evaluated by the previews in [Apply the selected transitions](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#apply-the-selected-transitions).\\n\\nBefore proposing a definition for approval, resolve its template placeholders.\\nIf it contains an `Open decisions` section, confirm that no unresolved decision\\nremains before setting that section to `None`. Do not remove an unresolved\\nquestion merely to make validation pass.\\n\\n**Completion:** The selected package has an explicit list of review findings\\nand approval blockers. No approval is inferred from these checks.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Resolve approval blockers](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#resolve-approval-blockers) resolves the blockers. [Obtain the human's decision](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#obtain-the-humans-decision) presents the package for a\\nhuman decision.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"2ea6b051236e13dd427317722d16ce7941ef5a4eb4789f7fdf568ab3d0cae3c0\"}, {\"heading\": \"apply-the-selected-transitions\", \"title\": \"Apply the selected transitions\", \"anchor\": \"apply-the-selected-transitions\", \"start_line\": 221, \"end_line\": 270, \"content\": \"### Apply the selected transitions\\n\\n**Inputs:** The exact artifact IDs, target states, accountable human identities,\\nreviewed file digests, decisions from [Obtain the human's decision](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#obtain-the-humans-decision), and clarification answers from\\n[Define the change](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#procedure) that must be retained in the transition reason.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** Only explicitly selected definitions and work orders\\n  (WO) receive the applied state and lifecycle-history entries.\\n- **Transient working material:** The preview and apply results, including\\n  any refused transition.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Confirm that the reviewed artifact contents still match the human decision.\\n2. Select only the named transitions. Approve governing definitions before\\n   dependent work, or use one explicitly selected packet for mutually\\n   dependent definitions when the evaluator permits it.\\n3. Preview the transition command below.\\n4. Compare every selected ID, target state, actor, and reported effect with\\n   the human decision. Resolve failed gates before proceeding.\\n5. Recheck reviewed definition digests immediately before applying.\\n6. Repeat the passing command with `--apply`. Do not add related artifacts\\n   that the human did not select.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set ARTIFACT-ID=TARGET --decision ARTIFACT-ID=ACTOR --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set ARTIFACT-ID=TARGET --decision ARTIFACT-ID=ACTOR --apply --json\\n```\\n\\nFor each clarification below the formal-decision threshold, add\\n`--reason \\\"ARTIFACT-ID=Question and confirmed answer\\\"` to both the preview\\nand apply command. Use the actual question and answer. When one artifact\\nhas several such answers, combine them into one reason value for that ID.\\nKeep each complete `ID=TEXT` value as one argument. Confirm the saved reason\\nin the resulting lifecycle entry before discarding the working notes.\\n\\nFor approval, `TARGET` is `approved`. For rejection, use `rejected` and add\\n`--reason \\\"ARTIFACT-ID=Rejection reason\\\"` to both commands. Rejection is\\nterminal. For a packet, repeat `--set` and `--decision` for every explicitly\\nselected artifact. Preview and apply must contain the same assignments.\\n\\n**Completion:** The evaluator reports the actual applied transitions, or the\\nspecific refusal. A successful preview alone changes nothing.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Confirm the authorization result](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#confirm-the-authorization-result) confirms the resulting authority and readiness.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"c5215dec9f35d1a99bf6e4d658a594a31dc544131e70db49b1b8f79514e124b8\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/AUTHORITY.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-digest39-20261012\\\\claude-351\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\AUTHORITY.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"da7202bf8d1beee631e9b4b5ba01c04b440fbf76d200ccbccbb8781a59f88431\", \"bytes\": 16163, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"decision-rights\", \"title\": \"Decision rights\", \"anchor\": \"decision-rights\", \"start_line\": 19, \"end_line\": 80, \"content\": \"## Decision rights\\n\\nA profile describes who performs an action. A decision right describes which\\naction that person or agent is authorized to perform. Being a Human does not\\ngrant every right in this table. Being an Agent grants none by itself.\\n\\nUse the artifact's recorded ownership and any existing explicit delegation to\\nidentify the accountable human. Resolve a team name or ownership label to the\\nhuman making this decision. A proposed `owners` value, an example actor name,\\nor authorship of a draft MUST NOT assign decision authority.\\n\\nApproval, verification acceptance, and release decisions MUST be made by an\\nauthorized human. An agent MAY prepare the material and apply that human's\\nrecorded decision when authorized to do so. Applying the decision does not\\nmake the agent its author. The selected IDs, reviewed content, target states,\\nand reason MUST still match the decision at apply time.\\n\\nThe following stable IDs name rights and operations, not additional profiles.\\nAll applicable gates remain required.\\n\\nThese DR-* identifiers come from the selected evaluator's decision-right\\ncatalogue. Use the right and exact action returned by that evaluator. Legacy\\nowner labels identify recorded accountabilities; they do not create additional\\nHuman/Agent profiles or assign a decision to a person by themselves.\\n\\n| Right ID                   | Accountable profile and authority                                                                                                  | Required input                                                                                                    | Result                                                                                               |\\n|----------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\\n| `DR-DEFINITION-DECIDE`     | Human authorized to decide the selected definition.                                                                                | Complete definition, exact proposed state, and required checks.                                                   | Decision on that definition only.                                                                    |\\n| `DR-WO-SELECT`             | Human authorized to approve the work scope.                                                                                        | Complete governing chain, bounded work order, and assurance classification.                                       | Approval or rejection of the selected work order.                                                    |\\n| `DR-WO-START`              | Human or Agent executing the selected work under its recorded approval.                                                            | Unchanged approved scope and passing start checks.                                                                | The selected work order becomes `in_progress`.                                                       |\\n| `DR-WO-COMPLETE`           | Human or Agent executing the selected work under its recorded approval.                                                            | Completed scope, passing handoff checks, and retained evidence.                                                   | The selected work order becomes `implemented`.                                                       |\\n| `DR-VREC-PREPARE`          | Human or Agent under the approval of every selected work order.                                                                    | Exact candidate, selected work and verification contracts, retained evidence, and passing preparation checks.     | One `ready` verification record; no acceptance decision.                                             |\\n| `DR-VREC-DECIDE`           | Human authorized to accept verification for the selected candidate.                                                                | Ready record, assessed evidence, and the exact verify, reject, or supersede decision.                             | Decision on that verification record only.                                                           |\\n| `DR-DELIVERY-SELECT`       | Human authorized for the chosen integration or release-preparation path.                                                           | Exact candidate, destination, and coverage required by that path.                                                 | One path-specific instruction.                                                                       |\\n| `DR-RLS-PREPARE`           | Human authorizes the release inputs; a Human or Agent may carry out the preparation.                                               | Exact release contract, candidate, included work, verification coverage, version, and passing preparation checks. | One `ready` release record; no release decision.                                                     |\\n| `DR-RLS-DECIDE`            | Human authorized to make the release decision.                                                                                     | Ready release record, assessed contract obligations, and the exact release or reject decision.                    | Decision on that release record only.                                                                |\\n| `DR-EXTERNAL-ACTION`       | Human authorized for the exact external action. A Human or Agent may execute the authorized operation.                             | Action, immutable target, destination, prerequisites, and recovery conditions.                                    | Authorization limited to that operation; the subsequent tool result records its actual effects.      |\\n| `DR-RELATED-RECORD-SELECT` | Human or Agent; no decision authority is needed for inspection.                                                                    | Exact existing artifact ID.                                                                                       | Read-only selection.                                                                                 |\\n| `DR-REMEDIATION-SCOPE`     | Human authorized to change the affected definition or work scope.                                                                  | Failed criterion and proposed bounded correction.                                                                 | Explicit authority for the revised scope.                                                            |\\n| `DR-DECISION-DISPOSE`      | Human accountable for the artifact blocked by the decision; for a deviation, the human accountable for the affected specification. | Open or deferred decision, selected option or disposition, exact reason, and any required revisit trigger.        | Recorded disposition and any declared paired-risk effect. Other blocked artifacts keep their states. |\\n\\nFor a lifecycle decision, the `--decision` value identifies its actual\\ndecision-maker. When an agent applies a human decision, that value is the\\nhuman who made it. For start and completion under approved execution, it is\\nthe actual Human or Agent executor. Supplying either identity does not grant\\nauthority. Preparation `--owner` values identify the preparation actor, not\\na future assurance or release decision-maker.\\n\\nFor `decide`, when an owner label differs from the actual human's identity,\\nuse `--authority-owner OWNER` only after establishing that human's authority\\nfor the exact decision. Keep `--decision ACTOR` as the actual human. The\\nevaluator checks that OWNER holds the existing decision right and records it\\nseparately as `authority_owner`. With the option absent, ACTOR must itself\\nmatch an eligible owner. The command does not authenticate either claim or\\ngrant consent. Do not infer a binding from the decision's own owners, an\\nearlier approval, Git configuration or the agent's identity.\\n\\nSilence, elapsed time, a commit, a pull request, tool execution, or a passing\\ncheck MUST NOT count as a human decision. A rejection MUST include a non-empty\\nreason. Supersession MUST name exactly one eligible successor. The decision\\nand its resulting state MUST remain specific to the selected artifact.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"6ecaeb29429bcaa4eac99857f2f921e270c4865d1edeec96f536d09eb6c3130a\"}, {\"heading\": \"delegation-and-separation\", \"title\": \"Delegation and separation\", \"anchor\": \"delegation-and-separation\", \"start_line\": 111, \"end_line\": 133, \"content\": \"## Delegation and separation\\n\\nA human decision right may be delegated only to another human who is permitted\\nto hold it. The delegation MUST name the right, its scope, the granting human,\\nand the receiving human. It MUST be recorded before the delegated decision.\\nThe receiving human MUST decide under their own identity.\\n\\nAn instruction for an agent to apply a recorded human decision delegates the\\nexecution of that command. It does not delegate the decision itself. The\\nagent MUST NOT supply a new answer, broaden the targets, or claim the human\\nmade a decision that was not given.\\n\\nRepository policy MAY require different people for conflicting decisions on\\nthe same candidate. When it does, the same human MUST NOT hold both decisions\\nfor that candidate. A human MUST NOT approve their own architecture decision\\nassessment unless they explicitly hold approval authority for that artifact\\nand the separation rules permit it. Drafting an assessment grants no such\\nright.\\n\\nWhen the accountable human or required authority is absent or ambiguous, stop\\nthe affected decision. State the exact right and artifact needing an owner.\\nDo not substitute an available human or agent merely to fill the field.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"f07389fbbe529ad544d8b7d7f18894bfa45f0ec69996c93ed8372416b55ddf91\"}]}]}",

[ARTIFACTS.md#artifact-types](ARTIFACTS.md#artifact-types) and [DEFINITION_LINKS.md#links-between-definitions](DEFINITION_LINKS.md#links-between-definitions).\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"3ce477685d52f9c993e2006dcb2e0e67c0beb152b3570f7b808f5af8367ec631\"}, {\"heading\": \"identify-the-existing-artifacts-that-apply-to-the-change\", \"title\": \"Identify the existing artifacts that apply to the change\", \"anchor\": \"identify-the-existing-artifacts-that-apply-to-the-change\", \"start_line\": 110, \"end_line\": 177, \"content\": \"### Identify the existing artifacts that apply to the change\\n\\n**Inputs:** The confirmed outcome, scope limits, any artifact IDs supplied\\nin the request, and the existing formal records. Use\\n`docs/engineering/README.md` when locating a domain.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** None created or modified.\\n- **Transient working material:** A list of selected artifacts (the artifact\\n  inventory), a list of missing definitions, and the evaluator results.\\n  These are needed only for the current change-definition context. The agent\\n  SHALL retain them in the conversation or, at its discretion, in a temporary\\n  location outside the repository. They SHALL NOT be persisted in the\\n  repository. The agent chooses whether a temporary file is needed and, if\\n  so, its filename and format.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Run the repository inspection command below.\\n2. If the request selects a work order (WO), run the selected-work command\\n   below. Read that work order (WO) and every file in the returned\\n   `context.reading_manifest`.\\n3. Otherwise, locate the artifacts named in the request. If none are named,\\n   use `docs/engineering/README.md` to locate the domain covering the function\\n   or component in the scope. Read its intents (INT), capabilities (CAP),\\n   and requirements (REQ). Record a missing domain if none covers the change.\\n4. Select the requirements (REQ) whose behavior the change would modify or\\n   reuse. Trace each one back to its capabilities (CAP) and intents (INT).\\n5. Read the specifications (SPEC), applicable architectures (ARCH), architecture\\n   decisions (ADR), verification contracts (VER), and work orders (WO) linked\\n   to those selected requirements (REQ). Use the declared relations listed\\n   in [Definition links](DEFINITION_LINKS.md#links-between-definitions). Keep unrelated work outside the selected scope.\\n6. Read decisions (DEC) and risks (RISK) that name the selected artifacts.\\n7. For each selected artifact, retain its name and code, such as Requirement\\n   (REQ), its ID, file path, current lifecycle state, and why it applies to the\\n   change. Keep this list as transient working material; do not create a\\n   repository inventory file.\\n8. Assign one treatment to each entry: `reuse`, `amend`, or `reference only`.\\n   Use `reuse` only when its existing meaning covers the change unchanged.\\n9. List missing definitions by type and purpose. Identify their intended domain.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\nRepository inspection, used in action 1:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl inspect REPO --json\\n```\\n\\nSelected-work inspection, used in action 2. Replace `WO-ID` with the actual\\nwork order (WO) ID:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\nBoth commands are read-only. `inspect` does not select the change scope.\\nThe checkpoint-free `check` reports lifecycle context without evaluating\\ncheckpoint gates. Preserve its reported blockers and next action.\\n\\n**Completion:** One inventory identifies the existing artifacts to reuse,\\namend, or consult and the definitions that are missing. Ambiguous artifact\\nidentities are resolved before dependent drafting.\\n\\n**Later use:** The inventory controls creation in [Draft any missing definitions](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#draft-any-missing-definitions), amendments in [Propose any needed amendments to existing definitions](AMEND_DEFINITIONS.md#propose-any-needed-amendments-to-existing-definitions),\\nand the verification, risk, decision, and work-order work in [Define how the result will be verified](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#define-how-the-result-will-be-verified), [Record risks (RISK) affecting the change](RISKS_AND_DECISIONS.md#record-risks-risk-affecting-the-change), [Record unresolved decisions (DEC)](RISKS_AND_DECISIONS.md#record-unresolved-decisions-dec), [Prepare work orders (WO) linking the definitions, scope, planned work, and verification requirements](DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md#prepare-work-orders-wo-linking-the-definitions-scope-planned-work-and-verification-requirements).\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"7b2f2d344ca3f55a24c469e2da9819fe937be5467541f815a2f53d2316b7c8b8\"}]}]}",

  "tool_use_id": "toolu_01DrWEvK1BtNa2T5vjDVQsat",
  "type": "tool_result",
  "content": "1\t# Private lifecycle rehearsal wire contract\n2\t\n3\tThe v2 endpoint accepts only `lifecycle-v2.json`. Every request and result selects\n4\ttest data. Git remains authoritative for real work. Existing v1 routes and the\n5\tseven read-only MCP tools keep their contract; they cannot dispatch v2 actions.\n6\tIn an explicitly configured test project their results use\n7\t`se-harness-graph-read/v2`, with a required test-copy label. Ordinary Phase 2\n8\tprojects retain their v1 read result shapes. Revision byte hashing is unchanged;\n9\ttest revisions add explicit test-copy provenance, without rewriting v1 fixtures.\n10\t\n11\t`inspect` accepts check, preflight and validate. `preview` runs a mutation in a\n12\tdiscarded projection and returns its input digest and complete proposed file\n13\teffects. `apply` requires that digest, reruns the released checks, and commits\n14\tthe complete result once. A preview never changes graph state. Actor strings\n15\tare synthetic test inputs, not authenticated human consent.\n16\t\n17\tThe digest binds project/context versions, all selected revision IDs, retained\n18\tsnapshot, exact command/file inputs, principal and released evaluator identity.\n19\tMode and operation key are excluded from the preview binding, but included in\n20\tthe accepted request digest used for principal-scoped retry lookup.\n21\t\n22\t## Permitted file effects\n23\t\n24\t| Action | Permitted effects in the disposable projection |\n25\t| --- | --- |\n26\t| check / preflight / validate | No file writes |\n27\t| transition | Only selected newly authored artifacts and evaluator sidecars declared by their records |\n28\t| decide | Selected new decision and its explicitly linked rehearsal risk |\n29\t| raise-risk | New risk and optional paired decision at released domain paths |\n30\t| handoff | Declared bounded file inputs within the selected work order's scope; released handoff packet and evaluator evidence |\n31\t| capture-verification | New VREC and its released evaluator sidecar |\n32\t| prepare-release | New RLS and its released evaluator sidecar |\n33\t\n34\tHandoff first uses the released procedure's fixed `evidence` preparation command,\n35\tthen `check --checkpoint handoff --from-git`. It exposes no arbitrary evidence\n36\tcommand. File inputs cannot alter formal records, harness selection/instructions,\n37\tGit configuration, hooks, or executables used by the service. The service never\n38\truns source tests. The local qualification runner supplies observed test evidence.\n39\t\n40\tThe adapter enumerates every regular file before and after the operation. It\n41\trejects deletions, links, unexpected paths, missing sidecars, imported-record\n42\tchanges and evaluator failure before opening an acceptance transaction.\n43\t\n44\t## Snapshots and Git\n45\t\n46\tEach accepted operation retains an immutable complete snapshot: exact file bytes,\n47\tself-contained Git history and its source/test-candidate identities. Content\n48\tdigests bind the snapshot to the resulting baseline. Context heads, artifact\n49\trevisions, snapshot and receipt share one database transaction. Temporary Git\n50\trepositories are discarded after the result is accepted or refused.\n51\t\n52\tCapture first commits the exact input projection as test candidate P. P excludes\n53\tthe new VREC. The output retains the input hosted baseline B, source fixture S,\n54\tP and evaluator identity. Actual implementation candidate C is recorded separately\n55\tby qualification. Subsequent commits never alter the candidate stored in a VREC.\n56\t\n57\tExport selects an immutable baseline, checks its complete snapshot and Git bundle,\n58\tand returns exact bounded bytes. The installed client writes only to a new explicit\n59\tdestination and leaves an incomplete marker until every file hash is checked.\n60\tNo export writes to or synchronizes an authoritative repository.\n61\t\n62\t## Bounds and failure categories\n63\t\n64\tKeep 4 MiB requests, 2 MiB responses, 1 MiB individual file inputs, two concurrent\n65\tprojections, 120-second evaluator calls and 10,000 retained artifact revisions.\n66\tA pilot snapshot/export that exceeds the response bound is refused atomically.\n67\tRetained complete snapshots have a separate explicit project count bound of 128.\n68\tThis is a small private pilot, not an unbounded document store.\n69\t\n70\tBoundary/malformed requests, stale inputs, evaluator refusals, invalid output and\n71\tunknown transport outcomes remain distinct. Read the operation receipt after a\n72\tlost reply. A changed request under the same accepted key is a conflict. The\n73\tservice does not automatically rerun a mutation at a newer version.\n74\t\n75\t## Bounded Cypher reads\n76\t\n77\tThe existing `cypher` MCP/HTTP read selects one exact view. Its request uses the\n78\tread schema, not the lifecycle command schema. Read the public labels and\n79\tproperties in [the physical representation](../../docs/notes/hosted-artifact-graph.md#physical-representation)\n80\tbefore selecting a query. `Artifact` and `Revision` are distinct public labels.\n81\t\n82\tThe admitted form has one `MATCH` or `OPTIONAL MATCH` path, labelled nodes,\n83\toptional parameterized property comparisons, `RETURN`, and a numeric `LIMIT`.\n84\t\n85\tRelationship paths are supported. Use `-[edge:TYPE]->` or `<-[edge:TYPE]-` between\n86\tlabelled nodes, with a public relationship type from the physical representation.\n87\tEach node and relationship needs a distinct variable name. Anonymous relationship\n88\tsyntax such as `-[:TYPE]->` is refused; a refusal of that form does not mean\n89\trelationship traversal is unsupported. Return-column aliases (`AS alias`) are\n90\toptional.\n91\t\n92\tFor example, with `parameters` containing a selected artifact ID under `artifact`:\n93\t\n94\t```cypher\n95\tMATCH (r:Revision) WHERE r.artifact_id = $artifact RETURN r.artifact_id AS id, r.status AS status LIMIT 10\n96\t```\n97\t\n98\tUse the response's completeness fields with the requested row/byte/depth budget.\n99\tThis example supplies no project filter: the service binds the selected project\n100\tand view. Unlabelled nodes, `WITH`, aggregation and arbitrary database syntax are\n101\tnot part of this small public read form. A rejected query has not completed a read.\n102\tKeep the exact refusal; consult the documented form before constructing another\n103\trequest. Do not weaken query admission or bypass it through a direct database call.\n104\t"
}

The Explorer shows this summary under the title.\noutcome = \"An operator can inspect greeting() and establish that it returns exactly Hello rehearsal.\"\n\n[relations]\n+++\n\n# Intent: Establish the exact rehearsal greeting\n\nSynthetic rehearsal input. This is not a real human decision.\n\n## In plain words\n\nAn operator needs to confirm that the fixture function greeting() returns the\nexact text `Hello rehearsal`.\n\n## Problem\n\nAn operator needs a fixed greeting for a disposable lifecycle rehearsal\n(INT-P3-900). Today no approved definition states that the operator can\nestablish the exact value that greeting() returns.\n\n## Success measures\n\n| Measure | Today | When reached | Observed |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| The operator inspects greeting() and establishes its exact return value | not measured | The return value is exactly `Hello rehearsal` | By the test operator, once in each disposable rehearsal |\n\n## Not this\n\n- No real product, release or human decision.\n"

## Event 399: assistant text

The service refused an approval apply once the draft content had changed. The refusal changed nothing, and a fresh preview then approved the amended draft once. This is test data in the sandbox only; Git remains authoritative, and `test-owner` is a synthetic fixture identity, not a real decision.

**What happened**
1. I imported the fixture, opened a draft context and wrote a new intent, **INT-P3-001**, for the greeting outcome. I picked an intent because it needs no upstream links. A requirement would have needed an active capability, and the only one, CAP-P3-900, is imported, draft and must not be changed.
2. I previewed its approval by `test-owner`. All gates passed, and the preview returned a digest (D1, `sha256:0ea933…`).
3. I amended the draft by adding the scope limit "No change to the greeting text or to `src/greeting.py`." This changed the selected revision from `0b108d…` to `899b1e…`.
4. I applied with the old digest D1 but the current project and context versions (5 and 3). The action was byte-identical to the preview. The service refused it with HTTP 409 `HAG_REMOTE_STALE_PREVIEW`: "Apply needs a preview bound to these exact inputs." Because the versions were current, the version check had nothing to reject; the digest check made the refusal.
5. The refusal committed nothing:
   - The response had no receipt, no affected revisions and no files.
   - Status still shows project version 5.
   - A Cypher read at context v3 still shows INT-P3-001 as draft at `899b1e…`.
6. A new preview (D2, `sha256:7eceed…`) passed, and its apply was accepted once. INT-P3-001 moved from draft to approved at revision `547b2ed…`, project version 6, context v4. A Cypher read confirms it.

**Identities**
- **Project:** `798f89d1-3b13-4183-a0ac-21f9a3fcfa36`
- **Source fixture:** `5820415…`
- **Evaluator:** 0.22.1
- **Client:** 0.22.2
- **Qualification candidate:** `ee86a842…`

Status matched the combination report before any change.

**Limits**
- **The digest's cause is not fully isolated.** The digest also covers the project and context versions, and the amendment advanced those as well. So this run shows the digest check, not the version check, refused the stale preview. It does not separate the content change from the version change inside the digest. Doing that would need a run where the versions advance without a content change, which I didn't do.
- **Cypher evidence files are my transcriptions** of the MCP results, not client captures.
- **The first preview had a wrong reason string.** The service adds the `INT-P3-001=` prefix itself, so my reason came out doubled. I kept that request unchanged for the stale apply so that only the draft differed, and fixed it in the second preview.
- **Four helper calls failed.** One `instructions` call rejected the reference-file paths, so I read those files directly. Three `read-json` calls failed because Git Bash rewrote the leading `/` in the pointer; the documented `'"/field"'` quoting fixed them. There were no permission denials.

**Not done**
- No work was started or completed.
- The greeting assertion and tests were not run.
- No verification or release record was prepared, and no risk was recorded.
- Nothing was exported or published, and the source was not changed.
- No decision was applied to the imported -900 records.

The evaluator's returned next step, "Decide the selected definition INT-P3-001 only.", was not taken.

Files are in `work/`:
- native-report.md
- native-observations.json
- numbered request and result records `01`–`15`
